and on the particular condition that the convict:

- for the duration of the operational period does not contact or is not contacted by - directly or indirectly - the (co)accused in this case: Imane B., Oussama C., Azzedine C., Jordi de J., Moussa L., Hicham el O., Hatim R. en Anis Z.;

- that the convict will refrain from utterances and/or publications in the public realm, which includes the Internet and (social) media, and/or lending his cooperation thereto;

dismisses the request to terminate the pre-trial detention order;

grants the temporary release of the convict, under conditions (separately recorded);

orders the return to the convict of the items specified in the list of seized objects, namely: a box of stickers (black with Arabic text); serial no. 1: Dell computer [product code 6]; serial no. 2: Western Digital, portable hard disc incl. USB cable; serial no. 3: Seagate Barracuda, internal hard disc 80 Gigabyte; serial no. 4: Asus computer g55vw, with power cable; flag with white text; flag, white, with black text.

25
Judgment - Jordi de J.

The court:

declares not legally and conclusively proved that the accused committed the offences charged under 2 first and second cumulative/alternative, and acquits the accused thereof;

declares legally and conclusively proved that the accused committed the offence charged under 1, and that the charge proven constitutes:

having intentionally procured for himself information to commit a terrorist crime and acquired the knowledge and skills to that end;

declares the facts proven and therefore the accused punishable;

does not find legally and conclusively proved any other charges than are declared proven hereinbefore, and acquits the accused thereof;

sentences the accused to:

a custodial sentence of 335 (THREE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FIVE) DAYS;

orders that the time spent by the convict in custody and pre-trial detention prior to the execution of this sentence shall be deducted entirely in the execution of the unsuspended part of the custodial sentence imposed on him, in so far as this time has not been deducted already from another punishment;

rules that part of this punishment, namely 180 (ONE HUNDRED AND EIGHTY) DAYS, is not executed on the general condition that the convict:

- does not re-offend within the operational period herewith set at three years;

- to establish his identity cooperates in the obtaining of one or more finger prints or offers for inspection an identity document as referred to in art. 1 of the Compulsory Identification Act;

- cooperates with probation supervision as referred to in art. 14d(2) DCC, including home visits;

and on the particular condition that the convict:

- for the duration of the operational period does not contact or is not contacted by - directly or indirectly - the (co)accused in this case: Imane B., Oussama C., Azzedine C., Rudolph H., Moussa L., Hicham el O., Hatim R. en Anis Z.;

- during the operational period reports to the Dutch Probation Service, address: Bezuidenhoutseweg 179, The Hague, at times determined by the Probation Service, as frequently and for as long as the Probation Service deems necessary;

- undergoes treatment during the operational period (i.e. cooperates in the intake procedure and the ensuing treatment plan) with Forensic Outpatient Clinic Palier, address: J. Westerdijkplein 40, The Hague, or a similar outpatient forensic institution, at the times and places indicated by that institution;

orders the Dutch Probation Service to supervise compliance with the above particular conditions and to provide guidance the convict for that purpose;

terminates the accused’s temporary release granted from pre-trial detention;

orders the return to the convict of the items specified in the list of seized objects, namely: Apple 4 telephone [product code 7]; Acer Aspire 5736z computer; laptop without charger and power cable; 2 flyers for street Da’wah.

25
Judgment - Moussa L.

The court:

declares not legally and conclusively proved that the accused committed the offences charged under 2 first and second cumulative/alternative in the indictment with Public Prosecutor’s Office No. 09/767238-14, and acquits the accused thereof;

declares legally and conclusively proved that the accused committed the offences charged in the indictment with Public Prosecutor’s Office No. 09/767238-14 under 1 first and second cumulative/alternative, and the offences charged in the indictment with Public Prosecutor’s Office No. 09/827053-15 under 1 first and second cumulative/alternative, and declares that the charges proven constitute:

with regard to the indictment with Public Prosecutor’s Office No. 09/767238-14:

with respect to charge 1, first cumulative/alternative:

having publicly, in writing or by image, incited others to commit a criminal offence, where the offence that is incited to is a terrorist crime, committed several times;

with respect to charge 1, second cumulative/alternative:

having disseminated written material and an image that incite to the commission of a criminal offence, knowing that they are inciting, where the offence that is incited to is a terrorist crime, committed several times;

with regard to charge 3:

slander, where the honour and reputation impugned are those of a public servant in the execution of his duties, committed several times;

with regard to the indictment with Public Prosecutor’s Office No. 09/827053-15:

with respect to charge 1, first cumulative/alternative:

having publicly, in writing or by image, incited others to commit a criminal offence and to violence against public authorities, where the offence that is incited to is a terrorist crime, committed several times;

with respect to charge 1, second cumulative/alternative:

having disseminated written material and an image that incite to the commission of a criminal offence and to violence against public authorities, knowing that they are inciting,where the offence that is incited to is a terrorist crime, committed several times;

with regard to charge 2 principally:

threat with a serious offence against the life of a person;

declares the facts proven and therefore the accused punishable;

does not find legally and conclusively proved any other charges than are declared proven hereinbefore, and acquits the accused thereof;

sentences the accused to:

a custodial sentence of 103 (ONE HUNDRED AND THREE) DAYS;

orders that the time spent by the convict in custody and pre-trial detention prior to the execution of this sentence shall be deducted entirely in the execution of the unsuspended part of the custodial sentence imposed on him, in so far as this time has not been deducted already from another punishment;

rules that part of this punishment, namely 60 (SIXTY) DAYS, is not executed on the general condition that the convict:

- does not re-offend within the operational period herewith set at three years;

- to establish his identity cooperates in the obtaining of one or more finger prints or offers for inspection an identity document as referred to in art. 1 of the Compulsory Identification Act;

and on the particular condition that the convict:

- for the duration of the operational period does not contact or is not contacted by - directly or indirectly - the (co)accused in this case: Imane B., Oussama C., Azzedine C., Rudolph H., Jordi de J., Hicham el O., Hatim R. en Anis Z.;

- that the convict will refrain from utterances and/or publications in the public realm, which includes the Internet and (social) media, and/or lending his cooperation thereto;

terminates the accused’s temporary release granted from pre-trial detention;

orders the return to the convict of the items specified in the list of seized objects, namely: a Samsung sm-T210 tablet.

25
Judgment - Hicham el O.

The court:

declares legally and conclusively proved that the accused committed the offences under 1 first, and second and third cumulative/alternative, and under 2 first and second cumulative/alternative, and declares that the charges proven constitute:

with respect to charge 1, first cumulative/alternative:

conspiracy to

murder with terrorist intent;

manslaughter, committed with terrorist intent and

intentionally causing an explosion where general danger to property and serious bodily harm or danger to another person’s life might have been expected to ensue from the act, committed with terrorist intent;

with respect to charge 1, second cumulative/alternative:

with intent to prepare and promote:

murder with terrorist intent;

manslaughter, committed with terrorist intent;

intentionally causing an explosion where general danger to property and serious bodily harm or danger to another person’s life might have been expected to ensue from the act, committed with terrorist intent;

to procure for himself or others the opportunity, means or information to commit that crime;

to have available items about which he knows that they are intended for the commission of that crime;

with regard to charge 1 third accumulative/alternative:

having intentionally procured for himself information to commit a terrorist crime and acquired the knowledge and skills to that end;

with respect to charge 2, first cumulative/alternative:

participation in an organization which has as its object the commission of terrorist crimes;

with respect to charge 2, second cumulative/alternative:

participation in an organization which has as its object the commission of criminal offences;

declares the facts proven and therefore the accused punishable;

does not find legally and conclusively proved any other charges than are declared proven hereinbefore, and acquits the accused thereof;

acquits the accused of any other charges referred to under 1 third cumulative/alternative in than takin g part in a training camp;

sentences the accused to:

a custodial sentence of 5 (FIVE) YEARS;

orders that the time spent by the convict in custody and pre-trial detention prior to the execution of this sentence shall be deducted entirely in the execution of the unsuspended part of the custodial sentence imposed on him, in so far as this time has not been deducted already from another punishment.

25
Judgment - Hatim R.

The court

declares not legally and conclusively proved that the accused committed the offence charged under 1 cumulative/alternative, and acquits the accused thereof;

declares legally and conclusively proved that the accused committed the offences under 1, second and third cumulative/alternative, under 2, first and second cumulative/alternative and under 3 first and second cumulative/alternative, and declares that the charges proven constitute:

with respect to charge 1, second cumulative/alternative:

with intent to prepare and promote:

murder with terrorist intent;

manslaughter, committed with terrorist intent;

intentionally causing an explosion where general danger to property and serious bodily harm or danger to another person’s life might have been expected to ensue from the act, committed with terrorist intent;

to procure for himself or others the opportunity, means or information to commit that crime;

to have available items about which he knows that they are intended for the commission of that crime;

with regard to charge 1 third accumulative/alternative:

having intentionally procured for himself information to commit a terrorist crime and acquired the knowledge and skills to that end;

with respect to charge 2, first cumulative/alternative:

participation in an organization which has as its object the commission of terrorist crimes;

with respect to charge 2, second cumulative/alternative:

participation in an organization which has as its object the commission of criminal offences;

with respect to charge 3, first cumulative/alternative:

having publicly, in writing or by image, incited others to commit a criminal offence, where the offence that is incited to is a terrorist crime, committed several times;

with respect to charge 3, second cumulative/alternative:

having disseminated written material and an image that incite to the commission of a criminal offence, knowing that they are inciting, where the offence that is incited to is a terrorist crime, committed several times;

declares the facts proven and therefore the accused punishable;

does not find legally and conclusively proved any other charges than are declared proven hereinbefore, and acquits the accused thereof;

acquits the accused of any other charges referred to under 1 third cumulative/alternative than taking part in a training camp;

sentences the accused to:

a custodial sentence of 6 (SIX) YEARS.

25
Judgment - Anis Z.

The court:

declares legally and conclusively proved that the accused committed the offences under 1 first, and second and third cumulative/alternative, and under 2 first and second cumulative/alternative, and declares that the charges proven constitute:

with respect to charge 1, first cumulative/alternative:

conspiracy to

murder with terrorist intent;

manslaughter, committed with terrorist intent and

intentionally causing an explosion where general danger to property and serious bodily harm or danger to another person’s life might have been expected to ensue from the act, committed with terrorist intent;

with respect to charge 1, second cumulative/alternative:

with intent to prepare and promote:

murder with terrorist intent;

manslaughter, committed with terrorist intent;

intentionally causing an explosion where general danger to property and serious bodily harm or danger to another person’s life might have been expected to ensue from the act, committed with terrorist intent;

to procure for himself or others the opportunity, means or information to commit that crime;

to have available items about which he knows that they are intended for the commission of that crime;

with regard to charge 1 third accumulative/alternative:

having intentionally procured for himself information to commit a terrorist crime and acquired the knowledge and skills to that end;

with respect to charge 2, first cumulative/alternative:

participation in an organization which has as its object the commission of terrorist crimes;

with respect to charge 2, second cumulative/alternative:

participation in an organization which has as its object the commission of criminal offences;

declares the facts proven and therefore the accused punishable;

does not find legally and conclusively proved any other charges than are declared proven hereinbefore, and acquits the accused thereof;

acquits the accused of any other charges referred to under 1 third cumulative/alternative in than takin g part in a training camp;

sentences the accused to:

a custodial sentence of 6 (SIX) YEARS.

Art. 126j (1) provides: ‘In case of suspicion of a serious offence the public prosecutor can order, in the interest of the investigation, that an investigating officer as referred to in art. 141(b) systematically obtains information on a suspect without it being apparent that he is acting as an investigating officer.’

This article provides as follows: ‘The police have the task, subordinate to the competent authority and in accordance with the applicable rules of law, to ensure effective law enforcement and to render assistance to those who need it.’

Paragraph 1 of this article provides as follows: ‘In case of suspicion of a serious offence the public prosecutor can order, in the interest of the investigation, that an investigating officer systematically follows an individual or systematically observes his presence or behaviour.’

Paragraph 1 of this article provides as follows: ‘In case of suspicion of a serious offence as referred to in article 67(1) which, in view of its nature or in conjunction with other serious offences committed by the suspect, constitutes a serious infringement of the legal order, the public prosecutor can order that an investigative officer as referred to in article 141(b) participates or assists a group of individuals, within which there is reasonable suspicion that serious offences are planned or committed, if this is urgently required by the investigation.’

This chapter gives an obviously summary description of the developments in Syria from early 2011 through September 2014. The court bases itself particularly on the (consecutive) expert reports of doctor Judith Jolen, a National Police Middle East expert (these expert reports are bundled in bundle 2 of the Framework case file of Operation Context, hereinafter: Jolen). These expert reports are wholly based on public sources, such as reports by the Independent International Commission of Inquiry on the Syrian Arab Republic of the United Nations Human Rights Council (IICIS), reports by Human Rights Watch and Amnesty International, journalist sources and websites, social media, documents and images of jihadi organizations active in Syria. Sometimes the court will refer directly to these underlying documents.

Article 83 DCC contains an exhaustive list of terrorist crimes. These crimes include various types of conspiracy and preparatory acts to certain terrorist crimes, as well as participation in and/or leading an organization that has as its object the commission of terrorist crimes. The term ‘terrorist object’ which is also derived from the Framework Decision, has been used in a number of existing serious offences for some time and there constitutes an aggravating circumstance (Cf. artt. 282b, 282c, 288a, 304a, 304b, 415a and 415b DCC)

The exclusion clause of the International Convention on the Suppression of Terrorist Bombings, 15 December 1997, reads: “The activities of armed forces during an armed conflict, as those terms are understood under international humanitarian law which are governed by that law, are not governed by this Convention, and the activities undertaken by military forces of a State in the exercise of their official duties, inasmuch as they are governed by other rules of international law, are not governed by this Convention.” (court’s emphasis)

As in article 1(4) of the International Convention on the Suppression of Terrorist Bombings, 15 December 1997

The public prosecutor’s closing speech contains an inconvenient carelessness in this respect. According to paragraph 7.5.4 Moussa L., when he was heard on 8 January 2015, allegedly stated to embrace the jihadist body of ideas; however, in this interview (Cf. Citroen 843) he denies this explicitly.

This concerns the transcription (rendered verbatim on the request of the defence) of the interview of Witness no. 1 of 15 January 2014. Hereinafter, only the relevant page numbers are stated, followed by the verbatim transcription (V1 is interviewing officer 1, V2 denotes interviewing officer 2, and AS denotes Witness no. 1).

P. 89

p. 47, verbatim: AS: Abu Jazid uhm known him for a long time, so uhm since uhm November 2012. Since that time, all the time I never really hung around with him, except uhm at the battle of the Hondius (...) I went, I hung around with him rather often ... to tell you the truth.

p. 29 and 30, verbatim: V1: Has he, it sounds as if he has a special status or something? AS: Yes, because he is very well known. Very well known... at the, at the mosque, too. (...) V1: And why is this then? AS: Uhm he gives uhm look, very often he is wearing the, those, the clothes. Not that the clothes mean much, but uhm he is very well known in the group. (...) And they are always sitting with him uhm he is the one who talks most almost (...) You often see him in the media as well.

p. 30, verbatim: Uhm you say he talks a lot, but what does he talk about, then? AS: About uhm he talks, lemme see, he he talks mostly about uhm ... about ehm, well, what was possible in the past, about the jihad of the past. (...) He talks about the jihad of the past and the prophet and how it was and such. (...) But this man should really gain some knowledge, he should also understand the past is not now. (...) The past was different. (...) V2: So the jihad of the past is what he often talks about ? AS: Yes. And sometimes he also talks about uhm Syria, and everything that’s going on there, for instance. Uhm this has happened and that has happened. Uhm this group has taken this (...) The Frees Syrian Army has uhm taken that town. And p. 63, verbatim: V1: What were those conversations about then? (...) AS: Uhm often about uhm the governing of the laws of Allah (...), about democracy and uhm nd uhm and such subjects. And uhm the leaders, present day leaders. (...) And that they uhm deserve, deserve to be rebelled against. They are no good leaders and uhm such like.

p. 30, verbatim: V1: Uhm to whom and in what kind of, what, on what kind of occasions, for instance? AS: Uhm, just at the, eating, or uhm walking around at the streets.

p. 62, verbatim: V1: And what is the last time that you were there? AS: The last time? That was, lemme see, at Frankies was a long time ago. Yes, not long, but perhaps about four months ago, or so. V1: Yes, yes. AS: Uhm yes, three months ago, sorry uhm three months ago.

p. 62, verbatim: AS: But at Fez (...) I was there last maybe uhm..... .uhm not that we sat down there, but I went there to take out some food, so maybe about three weeks ago. (...) Uhm when I sat down maybe, maybe that was a month ago.

p. 31, verbatim: V1: Well, what what, they, what kind of impression did he make, what do you think he thinks about it, about the struggle in Syria? AS: (..) He believes the struggle in Syria is well uhm uhm uhm should be over. That the Bashar should leave quickly and uhm uhm fighters should be in power soon. V1: Which fighter? AS: Well, ISIS. (..) And ISIS have come to help people, that is what he says.

p. 71, verbatim: V2: (...) Uhm yes, what, what, what else? What, what, what else did he do? What, what did, did [he] glorify it a little, saying it’s right, it’s good. AS: Yes, he glorified [it]. He says (...) uhm the soil of Syria is now, is uhm blessed and such.

p. 65, verbatim: V2: But what kind of things does he post about ISIS then uhm on YouTube? AS: Uhm, uhm, uhm lemme think, that ISIS liberates their prison like, or uhm ISIS uhm uhm uhm uhm supposedly children, (...) treats children well, treats well children and such videos like. V2: But uhm with violent videos showing beheadings and that sort of stuff .... come... AS: Uhm I have seen that he someone uhm uhm had opened a city, but with a bomb attack... a suicide terrorist. V1: You have seen a video of it? AS: Uhm yes? V1: How did you get this video then? AS: Uhm via YouTube, but uhm always says a name eH.  V1: And what name did he use then to upload videos onto it? (...) AS: Uhm uhm there is uhm lemme see uhm ... Ahlu-Sunna Publicatie if I am right. P. 66, verbatim: V2: And uhm but how do you know that Abu Yazeed posts them, those uhm...? AS: Well, he told me.

p. 72, verbatim: V1: Where did you watch those videos? AS: Uhm when he is at Frankies, or at Fez, then [he] just shows them to others, a group. OR uhm when he is with only me, than in the mosque. (...) V1: And how would he show them? (...) AS: Just on his phone. (...) Yes, suddenly there are, he would say uhm: Watch these videos. Just issued by ISIS, watch this. And I would just watch [them] along with him (...). He showed altogether, I won’t tell a lie, five videos, six videos.

p. 70, verbatim: V2: Uhm what did Abu Yazeed uhm say about the struggle in, taking part in the struggle in Syria? AS: (...) what i heard is uhm he would just, just show me a video. (...) He would say, like: these fighters are good, uhm may [they] be praised and such. Such (...) such things he would say to me. V2: And have any, what kind of videos were they? AS: Uhm that you on this one uhm you see ISIS fighters fight against the Bashar fighters (...) and that they win and [he] says look, they win, may we praise them. V2: Yes, so he supports ISIS, that much is clear. AS: Yes, clear. (...) V2: But (...), what else does he say about uhm the mujaheden about uhm ... AS: Yes, the mujahedeen. (...) yes, hij just thinks they’re good. He says uhm they get martyrdom and uhm they rate high with Allah. (...) More and they get the higher rank in paradise.

p. 71, verbatim: AS: (...) Because I I uhm because of him I watch these videos together with him and he says May Allah reward them. And he says: watch this carefully and stuff. Then he switches off the video and we uhm just talk about uhm other subjects.

p. 71

p. 35, verbatim: V2: But what about Jihad then, applying it wrongly? AS: Well, just that their Syria is Jihad.

p. 39, verbatim: AS: They say that their emir is uhm Baghdadi. V2: Who? AS: Baghdadi of ISIS, you know?

p. 40, verbatim: AS: Them believe that Shiites are not Muslims. And that you, you should help your brothers and sisters uhm uhm phy, physically or support them with money, or with clothers or uhm men, men. (...) V1: What do you mean with men? AS: Well, just that you go and fight. P. 41, verbatim: AS: And the brothers do not think that you go to Syria uhm and you must fight, and should grab your gun and fight. They say, well, you can also be used as uhm technology, or uhm ICT’er, or uhm cook, for such and similar things. (...) Uhm you need not always fight. That’s what they say, yo uknow, you do not always need to pick up your gun.

p. 67, verbatim: V1: Why do you call them extremists then? AS: (...) Uhm because they were hard in their belief. (...) V1: But what do you base that on, that they were too hard and too extreme? AS: Uhm for instance uhm takfirs (...) declare uhm people unbelievers uhm rebel, uhm go uhm uhm uhm rule by the law of Allah uhm is (...), them go too deep with that.

p. 69, verbatim: AS: (...) but look, they speak indirectly, not directly.

p. 12, verbatim: V1: This group of Abu Yazeed’s what uhm what uhm ... AS: Yes, it is about the martyr only, is not it.  V2: Only that. AS: Not only that, but uhm….. V1: But it does a lot. AS: ... mostly, yes.

Some examples in this connection (sometimes verbatim reproduction and sometimes succinct representation):- ‘you should visit Ask.fm’ (Hartig, p. 540);- ‘I keep quiet but sometimes [one must] be very hard to people in a civilised way so as to open their eyes’ (Hartig, p. 540);- ‘you are a democrat or a Muslim’ (Hartig, p. 548);- ‘that’s why I called you, because now I can make my statement more easily’, ‘that’s what we start out with, am I right or not?’ and ‘this is what we have to learn’ (Hartig, p. 552);- ‘I would say: watch out for the leader’ (propounding a certain message) (Hartig, p. 553);- ‘Oussama C. created a new Facebook account and he is going to tackle those people in his school’ and ‘I am going to respond to everything and we’ll drive them absolutely crazy’ (Hartig, p. 559);- ‘how can you keep your religion safe living under the laws of the kufar (unbelievers)?’ and ‘how can a Muslim allow that the laws of Allah, the greatest, are just disregarded ..., that is just unacceptable’, followed by ‘that is something you should doubt straightaway’ and ‘of course you should do anything to retrieve those laws’(Hartig, p. 561);- Oussama C. does not understand what went wrong that [Unidentified] thinks differently’ and ‘Oussama C. says that [Unidentified] is allowed to have doubts, but matters are different’ (Hartig, p. 561);- ‘Oussama C. says: give me that person’s e-mail address (who talked to [Unidentified] about her religious convictions), I will e-mail him or her’ and ‘this person is trying to mislead you’ (Hartig, p. 563).

Hartig, p. 1442

Mokka, p. 312: S: Is OK. I will ask him. And then send it to your Facebook. Y: No, my Facebook is no good man. Best send it to my mail. S: Oh.  Hatim R.? Y: Yes man. S: Is OK.

On 16 May 2013 Hatim R. was seen in the Netherlands. (Cf. Mokka, P. 282), which conflicts with the official report of 3 July 2014, reporting that he had been in Syria since March 2013, Mokka, pp. 256 and 257. Although there are more clues that Hatim R. probably departed before late May, this counter indication will be to his advantage. The entry made in Mokka p. 250 on 1 June 2013 appears to be mistaken considering the fact that the reference number contains 2012; this should obviously be 1 June 2012.

Anijs, p. 70

Anijs, pp. 65 and 66

Anijs, pp. 213 and 214: S: Saddik (phonetically rendered) is also inside. A: Yes, I heard. I told [Subject no. 21] (name rendered phonetically) (...) A: I heard Abu Yousef went back to Turkey S: Who? A: Abu Yousef is said to go back. He does not want to let on. But I heard it here. They were talking in Arabic and stuff. He has given the gun ... the emir. Now they refuse to give back the gun, because they’re telling him: “you want to go back to the Netherlands” and stuff. You know? S: Yes. Why does he want to come back? A: I don’t know. I heard he wanted to return and then he changed his mind and came back again. Maybe there is a reason, but I don’t know why. S: God only knows, but there may not be a reason, nothing. People who talk too much .... always talking tough, tough, tough, but when push comes to shove they cannot handle things, you know? (in the background with S. an unindentified male says: Like me, right?) That is why ...(unintelligible).. people who don’t talk too much, but just show action, you understand?

Anijs, pp. 216 and 217: A: Yes, well, but for such things you need money, brother. S: Yeah yeah yeah right. It will work out, inshallah. What was i going to say ... what were we talking about? .. Someone flashed me [sic]. There was a brother, I had been fundraising together with him for a long time, you know? I swear, it was very easy a sum with six noughts, very easy ... I had been trying for a long time together with him ... preparing for this and that. It would be very easy, he would just order things, and that would be it. Nothing to do with violence or stuff. Just order things, and that would be it. Then I heard he had already left. He is with you there now. Just like that. S. says he called the brother, but only got voicemail. S. says that he invested four months of his time and all of a sudden the brother no longer felt like it and switched off his phone and went there.

Anijs, p. 219: A. says he has just finished standing guard, guarding the door. It was his duty to stop every car driving by. Every car had to be asked to show an ID. Everything is closed off, no petrol and food are allowed through because there are PKK in the area and the PKK is going to attack them soon. A. says that he is not quite sure who are PKK, but if in doubt they question the individual. Recently he stopped a car full of barrels of petrol and it was not allowed to pass through. The man was not PKK, but there are also Sunnites there. (...) Soufiane asks whether he has heard from Saddik and Subject no. 21. A. says he has not heard a thing from Saddik. A did see Abu Ferris yesterday.

Anijs, p. 222: Soufiane asks: “how much do you get there per month?” A man: “I don’t know. That one time we were given by Bahdadi”. S: Do not mention any names. A: Eh? S: Do not mention any names! Do not mention any names.

Anijs, p. 223: Then A. says that Subject no. 18 and Abu Redouan have an apartment.

Zilt, p. 55

Zilt, p. 56

Anijs, p. 227: Anis Z.: A letter came that there is no Dawla (state) here anymore [this is a reference to Islamic State in Iraq and Sham - (interpreter’s note)] and that everyone must go to Jebhat (Front): [this is a reference to Jebhat annusra - (interpreter’s note)]. (...) Anis Z. says that brothers go to the “Jebhat” (Front) and that he thinks he will join them. Soufiane says: Everything just stays the same, is just a change of name, isn’t it? Soufiane asks who sent the letter, the Supreme One or the one below. Anis Z. the Supreme One.

Anijs, p. 255

Mokka, p. 311

Anijs, p. 87: F. says that Soufiane’s little brother is busy with the Quran and is doing well. Conversation continues about which Quran verses the brother had taken along. F. says that [he] has spoken to his sister, and she had asked if he is acting the Ninja. They laugh. Soufiane asks if things are going well between the brothers and whether the irritations have been resolved. Soufiane says that he heard that Subject no. 25 (phon.) and Abu Redouan have made up. F. says that he talked about Soufiane with the brother. Soufiane says the brother has called him and asked him for forgiveness. F. says: “I told him that such things via the mail ... if you pass on this kind of message, it can be interpreted very differently, you know. You should say things like these on the phone when he talks to you, or something? “F. says that on the phone emotions play a part as well. Soufiane asks “How are things between him and Subject no.18?” F. says they are alright.

Anijs, p. 89: S. asks whether F. has seen his little brother. F. says that he has heard that S.’s little brother is going to “the other brothers”. S. says this is true and adds: “I thought all of you?”  F. says: No, not me, I don’t think that’s wise now. F. says that things are a bit turbulent with the other brothers. S. says that Anis Z. said a letter had arrived in which the Supreme One had written that everything should be like it had been before. So the people from Sham for Sham and Iraq for Iraq. The Supreme One had ordered everyone to go back to their own groups, and this had been pronounced three weeks ago. F. says he is alright there now. There had been an argument as well, S. says. F. does not know anything. F: Still no reason to go back. I don’t notice anything, I am just here, quietly.

Anijs, p. 232

Anijs, p. 233

Anijs, p. 233: Soufiane asks Anis Z. to tell Subject no. 25 that he should check his e-mail. In the background A. shouts to Subject no. 25: “Subject no. 37 says that you should check your e-mail, he is waiting. ” Anis Z. says that Subject no. 25 says he has read the e-mail. Subject no. 25 says that he is far away from that brother.

Soufiane says it is important. Soufiane wants to know how long it will take before the brother will know. There is some urgency involved.  Subject no. 25 will get on the Internet tomorrow. Soufiane says he has just spoken to Subject no. 21 about the fitna that is going on. I have explained it a little to him. Anis Z. says: “Subject no. 21 is still with those others”.

Soufiane says that Subject no. 21 does not know what is going on and that he doubted whether the letter was real. A. says that Subject no. 21 is far away. News does not travel to the area where he is. That’s why Subject no. 21 does not know what’s in the letter. Anis Z. says: Call Subject no. 21 and tell him that he should go to thingummy, or that he calls me, and that I will go to him together with Subject no. 25 and stuff. Soufiane says: he said he is coming your way tomorrow or the day after to hear what’s going on.

Anijs, p. 234

Anijs, p. 143: S: How are you all the brothers doing? R: They are doing well. R. says that there is supposed to be some Fitna (arguments) between the groups. S. says he has heard. S. says that Satan visits those kind of places to cause arguments between the brothers. R: A bit chaotic. No one knows what to do. S: All you can do is say your prayers and do what you came to do. Dou you get me? R: That is the most important. Don’t deviate from your goal. S: Just a moment, just a moment. How are you all our brothers doing? R: Yeah alright, but some have changed to another group. The majority of the Dutch brothers go there. The majority of the Belgians have already joined them. S: Don’t they.  I spoke to Subject no. 21. He really did not know about the letter. He will drop by you today or tomorrow. He said he was going to get information from the brothers. (...) S. asks if most of the Dutch brothers have come along. This is confirmed. Good, Soufiane says. He says it is important that everyone goes together.

Zilt, pp. 30 and 31: S. says he is glad to hear R.’s voice. Soufiane Z. says that he is still at home. Soufiane Z. asks if the food is alright over there. Soufiane Z. further asks how all the brothers are doing, and how his little brother and Subject no. 21 are doing. R. says that S.’s brother has gone with the brothers from Delft. Soufiane Z. responds: “Has he gone AWOL?” R: He had better stayed with thingummy man, him and Subject no. 21. Until all of us were gonna go, you understand? But he is old enough to look after himself. S. asks if there was fitna (an argument).  R says: Yes, with Subject no. 21 and such .. (unintelligible) .. a day of just rebat, rebat (standing guard, conducting checks). S. says that his little brother (Anis Z.) was angry with the Emir because he had not treated them well. They had fought a ghazwa (battle) and had secured loads of ghanima (spoils) but other people who had justbeen asleep had been given that ghanima (those spoils). R. says: Yes, I know, I was there during the action. Soufiane Z. also asks if there is fitna (arguments) amongst the people.  R. says there is, and there was a lot of shooting at each other and that people are eliminated. R: I am waiting now, but I am also switching over to the others, inshallah ... (...) R. goes on to say that he has called the “somewhat older man” about the saaf (money), which has been distributed amongst brothers who did not really need it. There is some left, but nobody needs it yet. R. says they should not complain if they have 1,000 already.  S. asks R. to give some to his little brother. R. says that he thought S.’s little brother had over 1,000. S. says no, his little brother only has 150. That’s what his brother told Soufiane Z. the last time around. R. says: If he has only 150, we’ll give him 300. We give 300 to people who have nothing and 200 to people who still have some. R: We have already given over 10,000 in this way, you know. We started with the Dutch, and if someone is a really good brother, then you can give him some as well, you see?

Anijs, pp. 260 and 261: A: I am just going to pick up all your stuff from Abu Redouan’s and keep them at mine. S: Yeah, OK, inshallah, as long as it is just safe. All that matters is that it’s safe. Look, if it is safe at his, leave it at his (...) A: At any rate, Subject no. 18 has just, has just split with ABU REDOUAN, and he has given Subject no. 38 (phon.) and a Belgian guy who has nothing to do with it, given 200 Euros and he has pocketed 2 heads, and he’s also given ABU REDOUAN 2 heads. S.: I am gonna tell you ONE thing ... A.: And both have a massive Jeep. S.: Yesterday I, listen. I spoke to Abu Redouan yesterday. He called me, he told me, wollah, it is not easy, people are lying about me, he says: I haven’t taken anything, I have, I have divided [it] among 10 people, I have divided among 10 people. Anyone who had over 1,000 Euros he’d give 200 Euros and people who did not have anything he’d give 300 Euros. He said I’ve already divided it among 10 people, I did not keep anything to myself. Because I asked him: have you given any to my little brother. He said: I believe your brother has 1,000 Euro, hasn’t he? So I told him, no, my little brother hasn’t, wollah, I really did not know, then I’ll give your brother some too, inshallah.  He says: I am still dividing it up. But he did not keep anything to himself.

Anijs, p. 94

Anijs, p. 95

Anijs, p. 89: S. asks: Have you heard from my little brother, or have not you? R: I am going there now, directly. I’ll be there in fifteen minutes. S. asks whether he has spoken to his little brother already. R. says: Yeas I have, I’ll tell you in a minute

Zilt, p. 70: S: Saddiq, he is with the Mujaherrien Brigades, with all the Moroccans. R: Good man, R. says that he wants to pay a visit to Saddiq one of these days. S. says he has Saddiq’s telephone number. R. says: Give me, give me. S: 0968489271. R: I’ll call him straightaway.

Anijs, pp. 97 and 98: F. says: Do you know whom I have found? Saddiq, man. S. asks if he has found him on his own or through others. F: No, I spoke to Abu Redouan. S. says: I gave it to him. F. asked the Emir and can go see Saddiq tomorrow. F. says that there are Brothers of the Muhajerreen (migrants) with him, F. S. says that he has heard that Saddiq is with 90% Moroccans. S. says: A good opportunity to train your combat skills with Saddiq. F. wonders why Abu Redouan knew (about Saddiq) before he did.  S. says that he spoke to Abu Redouan yesterday and that he had given him Saddiq’s number. S: Then I thought: I am going to call Subject no. 21, but I got your voicemail. (...) S. asks whether he (Saddiq) is far away from him.  F. confirms this. F. says that tomorrow he is going to Abu Redouan first, and then they’re going to Saddiq together.

Anijs, p. 263

Anijs, p. 264

Anijs, p. 101: S: You just saw Subject no. 24 (phon.). Did you chill a little with him? F: Yes man, I chilled with him before he uhm.. he siad to me: this is the last thingummy for me man. He knew it would be the last time man. That it would be his last fight [interpreter]. I sat with him, together with a brother. He said: Listen, you must divide this up [amongst] so and so. S. says that he read his last will. S. says that the last will contained something about his mother, his father and little brother. To his father he had said: If you want to know anything about me the go to the brothers whom I love most and who meant a great deal to me in my life. And these brothers are Abu Redouan, Subject no. 18, Subject no. 21. Subject no. 38 (phon.) and Subject no. 37 (...) F. asks how subject no. 25 is doing. Soufiane Z. says that Subject no. 25 was with his little brother and with Abu Redouan. F. says that he has not heard from Subject no. 25 for a long time. S. asks whether F. has seen his little brother. F. says he may see him tomorrow.

Zilt, p. 81: S: I have been round and I have left a short message. R: I will respond as soon as possible. S: Alright, it is only a very short message. If there is anything, you should keep me posted inshallah. S: Have you buried Subject no. 22 already? R: I think they have, I am telling you, they are at a distance from us, you see. R. says he has not seen Subject no. 22. Other brothers have, they are with him now. S. asks if it happened today. R. says: Yes, this morning. All the brothers are doing alright.

Anijs, p. 103: S. congratulates [interpreter] F. with Subject no. 39. F. had not seen Subject no. 22 anymore, he or Subject no. 22 were in Haleb and F. was en route. F. has not attended the funeral. F. says that the other brothers may have taken pictures. (..) F. says that he has seen Saddiq. He is on his own somewhere, but may come “this way”, where F. is as well. First to pay them a visit but maybe also to stay.

Zilt, p. 86

Anijs, p. 110: S: Are you still doing iqtihaam (invasion, forcible entry) and ribat (standing guard, conducting checks) and the like? F: I am just waiting inside a house. You have to await your turn and then Buschra (good news). S: Masha’allah. F: And your little brother, how is he doing? S: He was on his way to ikhtihaam (invade, enter by force), exactly where Soufiane Z. became shadeed (martyr).

Mokka, p. 320: S: Inshallah (phon.) What are you doing? Y: unintelligible. S: What did you say? Y: Making a list for the brothers who must do something (...) NN: Have you seen Subject no. 21? Y: No, I spoke to him yesterday.

Mokka, p. 323: F. says that he tried to get hold of Abu Yusef, but did not get through to him. F. asks S. to pass on a message to Abu Yusef: Whether he will keep F.’s stuff that’s there. His laptop is there as well.

Mokka, p. 326: S: Is OK. Did you speak to Abu Yusef? F: No man, I will give him a call in a minute. I have to top up and then I can call with this number. S: Can’t you.  No, I had already told him anyway that he should go and collect the amana/what was left in his care and stuff. So ... F: No, inshallah, brother is going to bring it round inshallah. S: Is he going to drop it off? F: Yes, I told him ... he is going to drop it off here, man. S: Masha’allah. F: That bag (phon.) ... S: What did you say? F: I gave him that bag and stuff, that kalash (phon.) I have taken for on the road you know.

Anijs, pp. 193 and 194: S: Did you manage to get hold of the brother? R: Which brother? S: Yes, the one my little brother was on about? R: Yes, I tried to get hold of him but I can’t at the moment. S: Because I am on my way to him now, to ... R: His number is ... S: Yeah? ... R: His number is switched off. Just off, altogether. S: Ah. ... inshallah. R: I am trying to get hold of him, you know. R: When I rang him yesterday it was ringing straightaway. S: Yes. Alright, what I’m gonna do, I’m gonna drive to a brother now, I’ll just see what’s what, because I think HE is in touch with him. And then I’ll give you a ring anytime within 45 minutes from now, on this number. R: ‘Cause that other uhm... that other [Subject no. 40] brings him in. S: Oh, I did not know that, you see. R: Do you get me? S: Yeah yeah yeah. R: So there’s someone else now. A new one, you see, so he must be ...uhm.  Well, he must be given a ring, he should be patient, that he should call me, so I can give him everything and then I could also go and pick him up. S: Yeah yeah yeah, of course. R: And maybe he´s already called that man, maybe that man has already taken him across the border. And maybe he´s just wandering around uhm ... near the border or something, you know ..... R: And when he´s brought in he´s gonna be taken to the Muhajireen Shaam anyway, he´s gonna be taken there. )...’ R: OK ..... inshallah.  Try and get hold of him. If you don’t succeed let me know. S: Yeah ... R: If not I’ll just drive over there I think to see if he’s there. S: OK, alright. I’ll call you within 45 minutes, within the hour I’ll give you a ring inshallah.  Yeah ... on this number. R: OK.

Anijs, p. 196: S: Brother, I’ve tried just now but it is true that his phone is off now, man. R: Yeah, that’s right, so I don’t know now if uhm... it is an hour by car, you know? S: Look, you know what it is, the brother I just paid a visit to has spoken to that brother last night, but he also said: his phone is off now. R: Yes ... S: So I am telling you straight, it’s very likely that he’s already in Syria, but that he’s across the border there, you know. R: Yeah, yeah, yeah, well, I am just gonna go home now. I’ll just check what the circumstances are, and then I might drive over there to see if he’s there. S: That’s OK. It’s important, ‘cause me personally I think he’s with them - the House of al Muhajireen (the migrants), or somewhere close to the border, you know.  Go and have a look inshallah.

Anijs, p. 199: A: That brother’s called me, from a different [unintelligible]. S: Can’t hear you. A: He is in ... [unintelligible] and Abu Redouan is going to pick him up. S: O. has he called? A: Yes, he’s called me.

Anijs, p. 203: S: Have you succeeded? R: Yes I have succeeded. He is with me. (...) An unidentified male gets on the phone [presumably the new recruit]. (...) S: Do call back home as well. NN: Yes, I have done already.

Anijs, p. 209: F: Soufiane Z.? S: You know Soufiane Z. pizza? F: Soufiane Z. who? S: Soufiane Z. pizza, the brother who gave you the glove. His brothers have this pizza thing, like. F: No way, is he shaheed? S: Yes man, he became shaheed last Saturday. F: Allahu akbar ... [unintelligible] ... S: Eh? F: At which fight? S: I don’t know, he had to do ribat (stand guard and do checks) and then ikhtihaam (invade, entry forcibly) and then he was shot there, then he became shaheed (martyr)

there inshallah.

This also puts in perspective which value should be attached to Rudolph H.’s indignation at the trial when he was related to this film, as he intentionally posted a link to the new Shaam al-Ghareeba page (3) after Shaam al-Ghareeba II had been taken offline by Facebook, which was obviously done for a reason (cf. also: Caramel, p. 1350). Subsequently, less than eight hours later this film was posted - again. Moreover, on 27 July 2014 Rudolph H. retweeted links to parts 1 through 4 of #Salil_as_swarim (cf.: Official police record of findings, supplement De Ware Religie / Shaam al-Ghareeba, Shaam Nieuws, appendix 6, p. 4).

Transcription (verbatim) of the interview of Witness no. 1 of 15 January 2014. Hereinafter, only the relevant page numbers are stated, followed by the verbatim transcription (V1 is interviewing officer 1, V2 denotes interviewing officer 2, and AS denotes Witness no. 1).

p. 37, verbatim: V2: Well er, that group we’re talking about right, how large is that group approximately ... AS: Er... V2: ... in The Hague, like? (...) AS: Those extremists? V2: ... yes (...) AS: I only know 20 (twenty), maybe 15 (fifteen) [of them] (...) V1: And where did you see those 15 (fifteen) or 20 (twenty) that you know of the extremist group, where did you see them? AS: All at the battle at Hondius.

p. 47 and 48, verbatim: AS: (...) That was wrong, they hung up flags, and you just should not do that. The flags were put up er look, and yes, they were there the whole time until I er and my brothers had this bandana ... (...) V2: What did it show again, on these er? (...) Yes, Tawhid, yes.

p. 50, verbatim: V2: ... and Abu Moussa was your spokesman then mostly, to the police as well, was not he? AS: Yes, exactly.

p. 51, verbatim: V2: Is it correct that at the battle, battle of Hondius, we saw Abu Yazeed there, and that he held a speech? AS: Yes, that’s right. V2: OK. AS: Because er we were in a circle and he was at midnight holding a speech. (...) AS: He was going to make a speech earlier about er I thought, I thought rather about the Jews, , but because of emotions he said: Right, we should not be afraid. We must just stand firm. (...) AS: And the speech itself was also about brother hood, sorry er, that we should help each other if anyone is attacked. But then he also means our brothers who are not in Syria, perhaps also in Syria, you never know.

p. 48, verbatim: AS: (...) They asked for our IDs, but nobody wanted to show them, they didn’t want to. (...) Er finally they had surrounded us and 1 (one) of the brothers then stood up [and said:] you know what: we’ll give them our IDs and then we just walk away from here. V2: Who said that? AS: That was Abu Said. Abu Said, that Dutch brother with the long hair (...) AS: Yes. Er er he said er just er er you should, you should just get up, just give your ID to the police and off you go.

p. 62, verbatim: V1: 9...) Er can you tell us who get together at Frankies? (...) AS: Er Abu Moussa at at Frankies, do I ... (...) AS: Er Abu Moussa, er, Abu Moussa Jordi de J. (...) AS: ... er Abu Yazeed (...) AS: ...and let me see er Abu el Soufit, no Abu Mohammed, Soufiane with the long, long hair (...) AS: Er let me see er Hakan, Turkish brother er ... and those are the, yes that, that is definitely their names, right? (...) V1: And when is the last time that you were there? AS: The last time? That was, lemme see, at Frankies was a long time ago. Yes, not long, but perhaps about four months ago, or so. V1: Yes, yes. AS: Er yes, three months ago, sorry er three months ago.

p. 62, verbatim: AS: But at Fez (...) I was there last maybe uhm..... .uhm not that we sat down there, but I went there to take out some food, so maybe about three weeks ago. (...) Uhm when I sat down maybe, maybe that was a month ago. V1: OK. And who meet up at Fez? AS: That will be Abu Yazeed er Abu Jordi de J. (...) ... er Abu Ghital er (...) AS: Er Awat very occasionally goes there. Er let’s see Abu Harith, that very good guy ... AS: Moussa Abu Ilyas he’s called.

Cf. chapter 14

p. 37, verbatim: V1: Have you seen them lately, outside? (...) AS: Demonstrations er the most recent demonstrations at er embassies and p. 85, verbatim: V2: Have you never gone along to Belgium? AS: Er I have once. That was er 2 (two) months ago. (...) AS: If I am right er we went there for a er, someone had invited us to dinner. All the brothers from Belgium went there. (...) AS: And I was with, let’s see with er, the car had, let me see whether I, Abu Ilyas (...) Abu Said was the driver and the man in front there was was er Abu Ghital. (...) AS: Abu Harith was there as well. (...) AS: You mean how long we were there for? For 3 (three) hours, I think, 4 (four). P. 86, verbatim: AS: (...) only 3 (three) things I attended, just that protest, the Hondius and er ... yeah the protest, Hondius and that in Belgium.

p. 64 and 65, verbatim: V1: Well take Abu Moussa for instance; what does he think about democracy? (...) V1: And ISIS for instance, what does he think of that? AS: Er ISIS, eh, I never hear him talk about it, he only has Facebook. (...) just like Abu Yazeed, just about ISIS and such on er YouTube. So er Facebook they’re more open and er...

p. 81, verbatim: V2: Do you know the site Shaam al-Ghareeba? AS: Er I know one that knows a Facebook page. V2: Yes. AS: Had it on Facebook once, I had when I came across it.

p. 39, verbatim: AS: They say that their emir is er Baghdadi. V2: Who? AS: Baghdadi of ISIS, you know?

p. 40, verbatim: AS: Them believe that Shiites are not Muslims. And that you, you should help your brothers and sisters er er phy, physically or support them with money, or with clothes or er men, men. (...) V1: What do you mean with men? AS: Well, just that you go and fight. Because he believes that er brothers are 1 (one) body. P. 41, verbatim: AS: And the brothers do not think that you go to Syria uhm and you must fight, and should grab your gun and fight. They say, well, you can also be used as er technology, or er ICT’er, or er cook, for such and similar things. (...) Er you need not always fight. That’s what they say, you know, you do not always need to pick up your gun.

p. 64, verbatim: AS: (...) And they hoped that Syria would be 1 (one) country with er a government, a government that er the laws of Allah

p. 69, verbatim: AS: (...) sometimes he talks about jihad, but look, them speak indirectly, not directly.

p. 67, verbatim: V1: Why do you call them extremists then? AS: (...) Er because they were hard in their belief. (...) V1: But what do you base that on, that they were too hard and too extreme? AS: Uhm for instance uhm takfirs (...) declare uhm people unbelievers uhm rebel, uhm go uhm uhm uhm rule by the law of Allah uhm is (...), them go too deep with that.

p. 70, verbatim: V2: Er what did Abu Yazeed er say about the struggle in, taking part in the struggle in Syria? AS: (...) what I heard is er he would just, just show me a video. (...) He would say, like: these fighters are good, er may [they] be praised and such. Such (...) such things he would say to me. V2: And have any, what kind of videos were they? AS: Er that you on this one er you see ISIS fighters er fight against the Bashar fighters (...) and that they win and [he] says look, they win, may we praise them. V2: Yes, so he supports ISIS, that much is clear. AS: Yes, clear. (...) V2: But (...), what else does he say about er the mujahedeen, about er ... AS: Yes, the mujahedeen. (...) yes, he just thinks they’re good. He says er they get martyrdom and er they rate very highly with Allah. (...) More and they get the higher rank in paradise.

p. 72, verbatim: V1: Where did you watch those videos? AS: Er when he is at Frankies, or at Fez, then [he] just shows them to others, a group. Or er when he is alone with me, then in the mosque. (...) V1: And how would he show them? (...) AS: Just on his phone. (...) Yes, suddenly there are, he would say er : Watch these videos. Just issued by ISIS, watch this. And I would just watch [them] along with him (...). He showed altogether, I won’t tell a lie, five videos, six videos.

p. 70, verbatim: V2: Er what did Abu Yazeed er say about the struggle in, taking part in the struggle in Syria? AS: (...) what I heard is er he would just, just show me a video. (...) He would say, like: these fighters are good, er may [they] be praised and such. Such (...) such things he would say to me. V2: And have any, what kind of videos were they? AS: Er that you on this one er you see ISIS fighters er fight against the Bashar fighters (...) and that they win and [he] says look, they win, may we praise them. V2: Yes, so he supports ISIS, that much is clear. AS: Yes, clear. (...) V2: But (...), what else does he say about er the mujahedeen, about er ... AS: Yes, the mujahedeen. (...) yes, he just thinks they’re good. He says er they get martyrdom and er they rate very highly with Allah. (...) More and they get the higher rank in paradise.

p. 39, verbatim: V2: What does Abu Moussa about that, for instance? (...) AS: He would talk about it er he would say what Jihad is and er what it entails and er how it should be fought and so on. (...) And er but Jihad in Syria, he believes that there is Jihad there. That is his opinion, that’s what he thinks.

p. 68, verbatim: V2: Just now you mentioned the name Abu Mohammed, right? AS: Yes. V2: ... Soufiane Z.? AS: Yes. (...) But he explained about, about what? AS: About mullahs who are opposed to the Jihad. V2: Yes, was it ... AS: Well hu hu kasa, he said they were oppressed and Saudi Arabia, ‘cause he does not believe it. Er and yes he said, those people say that those mullahs are oppressed to be against the jihad.

Cf. chapter 16

Statement by Azzedine C. at the trial

p. 76 and 77, verbatim: V2: How does it work? Who helps them? Who helps er these guys er then? AS: Er to be honest, I don’t know. I try to find out. I do try to find out, but no one talks or says anything. They, as if they protect them and themselves, as if er don’t want to betray, or when you really want to go, then this person can help you, but I really don’t know. Maybe the brothers er raise funds for this er or er er lemme see er give names of brothers in Syria. When you are there you are collected, or how you should go, stuff like that. (...) AS: And I ‘ve asked recently er, I said for instance er like er: How do people get to Syria? They just say: via Tur, the Turkish border. But hey never want to tell how, they won’t say that. I don’t know why, but maybe they are cautious or something. Maybe Abu Yazeed does not know himself. Perhaps someone else in that group, it happens, of someone who er never frequents that group.

Caramel, p. 566

Caramel, p. 564

p. 43, verbatim: Er and the guys who eventually Soufiane wanted, wanted to go apparently. AS: Yes. V2: But what, what happened before then. Abu Yazeed saw a lot of him You saw him sometimes, as well. (...) V2: ... can you tell how this worked? I told Soufiane: Do you want to go to Syria, too? V2: Yeah, yeah, yeah, yeah, yeah. AS: Because well, he said er, so I want to go there, I know it is normal or eh.  Until I was, I was very new to that er thing. Then I said, like: Yeah, that er should, should be talked about under the table really. So that is all discussed under the table. So in other words: Maybe, right, maybe those brothers have not talked to other people to them under the table? Because [he’s] never talked about this subject out in the open, maybe he’s gone to Syria eventually. I think so, that’s what I suspect. Because he said himself that one discusses this er this kind of subject under the table. (...) AS: Because, in other words er, that they er are alone, no telephones er er and [are] in places nobody knows that they are there, then maybe they’ll talk and stuff. Where maybe other brothers are there and stuff. V1: Mm, mm. AS: That’s what I suspect. (...) AS: And he says well, you should not, you should not ask about stuff like this, you know.  Only discussed under the table that’s discussed, under the table. V2: That’s what he, what he said to you? AS: Yes, that’s what he said to me. V1: But how did the subject come up then? AS: Er I had, I also saw those guys. V1: Mm, mm. AS: And and er I, well, I, I already knew this group had something to do with Syria. And I asked him, well, Maybe you also want to go to Syria?
